The earlier draw of this set asked thirty questions ($n=90$, ten positives per state) and reproduced the naming boundary.
Under the summary that names only superseded content the named state scored $0.6550$ to $0.8275$ across the three models, while the two counted states scored $0.5225$ to $0.6225$.
At that size GPT-4.1 mini reached $0.5939$ against $0.5000$, with an item-level interval of $[-0.0345,+0.2315]$ that includes chance and a question-clustered interval of $[+0.0325,+0.1625]$ that excludes it.
The set could therefore neither establish the aggregate effect nor establish contamination, and the $444$-item form was built with the power the first lacked.
On that form the all-states lookup exceeded three monitor rows by more than the tolerance: GPT-4.1 mini by $+0.1947$ under the first summary and $+0.1467$ under the second, and Haiku 4.5 by $+0.1480$ under the first.
The name lookup exceeded GPT-4.1 mini by $+0.0322$, inside it.
The struck clause of that form's failure text is in the supplement (Section~S.6).
Of the $204$ questions drafted for the set and its replacements, $111$ name the plan or value their attribute is about, against $3$ of $30$ in the earlier draw.
A question is identical across the three versions of its store, so what it names cannot carry the label, and Appendix~\ref{app:posthoc-flags} splits the set by that flag.
The summary-leakage screen returned $0$ of $30$ on the all-states summary and $0$ of $30$ on the totals-randomized one.
The draw replaced $45$ pairs whose successor record failed our read and closed at $88$ supersession pairs (Table~\ref{tab:registrations-b}).

\paragraph{Coverage on the counterfactual set.}
The coverage score was recorded in the same calls, so the set also measures coverage under the same identity.
One blind score per question, reused across its three versions, gives blind FOK $0.5000$ by construction, and the positives are the \present{} and \pn{} versions ($296$) against the \absent{} version ($148$).
Table~\ref{tab:cf-fok} gives each model's coverage AUROC under the three summaries with the interval grouped by persona, and every interval excludes $0.50$.
Opus 5, whose scores take about $200$ distinct values, separates the versions more than the two smaller models, whose scores crowd a dozen values.

\paragraph{The lexical lookup on this set.}
The similarity lookup of the main text is the embedding variant.
The lexical variant, token overlap between the question and the names, reads the same lists (Table~\ref{tab:lex-444}).
It reaches $0.6698$ on the whole set under the superseded-only summary and $0.8085$ under the all-states summary, below the embedding variant by $0.10$ and $0.07$.
Under the superseded-only summary every model exceeds it with intervals that exclude zero, so the lexical reader is the weaker of the two, and the main text's comparisons use the stronger.

\paragraph{A hybrid reader with no model in it.}
%% R124 E1 (2026-09-19): E10, registrations/ADDENDUM_CF2_HYBRID_READER.md frozen at fe6ff84 (reviews/v21/FREEZE_E10.md), scored by
%% r82_counterfactual/tools/cf2_hybrid.py; r82_counterfactual/cf2/NUMBERS_CF2.md section 14e, AUX-CF2-HYB-*; no model call. The registration's
%% section 6: in every branch this table prints; its Section 5 sentence lands by ruling and is held (REPORT_R124.md).
A logistic regression of the label on the name lookup's cosine, the category-guessing reader's share and an indicator that a category was assigned, fitted under leave-persona-out and scored out of fold, reads $0.7865$ on the whole set under the superseded-only summary.
That is $+0.0201$ above the name lookup, with the item-level interval $[-0.0016,+0.0410]$ including zero and the persona interval $[+0.0039,+0.0409]$ excluding it, so the registered prediction that the combination exceeds the lookup reliably failed.
GPT-5.6 Sol stays above it by $+0.0635$ with both intervals excluding zero, as predicted; Opus 5 and GPT-5.6 Luna are reliably above it as well, Haiku 4.5 sits within tolerance of it, and GPT-4.1 mini is reliably below (Table~\ref{tab:cf-hybrid}).
The combination gains on the two counted states ($+0.0889$ on retraction and $+0.0942$ on expiry over the lookup's $0.5000$) and reads $-0.0153$ against the lookup on supersession, where the lookup alone scores $0.9223$.
%% R131 (2026-09-20): the hybrid under the matched metric (AUX-CF2-R125-MATCHED-V2 HYBRID_v2; -CONTRASTS 'hybrid minus name lookup (P-H1)', 'Sol minus hybrid (P-H2)').
Under the matched metric the same reader reads $0.8851$ ($114/34/0$), $+0.1081$ above the name lookup with intervals $[+0.0777,+0.1419]$ and $[+0.0741,+0.1419]$, and the three stronger models are level with it at the point: GPT-5.6 Sol $-0.0270$, GPT-5.6 Luna $-0.0068$ and Opus 5 $-0.0101$, each with both intervals including zero, while Haiku 4.5 ($-0.1081$) and GPT-4.1 mini at five draws ($-0.1588$) are reliably below it.
So the two registered predictions, read under the pooled metric, reverse under the matched one, which was defined afterwards: the combination is reliably above the lookup within triples, and Sol is not reliably above the combination.
Table~\ref{tab:cf-classes} shows where the pooled metric's ordering comes from: the hybrid's within-triple and same-state values are close to the three models', and its across-state value is not.

\paragraph{The sibling-name control.}
%% R131 (2026-09-20): R125 Task 4 (AUX-CF2-R125-SB-*).
The matched comparison still lets a reader profit from list length, since the \pn{} version's superseded-names list is one name longer than its siblings' and $24$ of the $88$ \present{} lists are empty (Table~\ref{tab:cf-emptiness}).
The sibling-name control removes that: a fourth store version per supersession pair carries the \pn{} version's text with the target's name in the superseded-names list replaced by another attribute of the same category, so the two versions differ only in which name is listed (Table~\ref{tab:cf-sibling}).
The name lookup separates the \pn{} version from it at $0.9489$ ($82/3/3$), and the five models at $0.8807$ to $0.9716$, none reliably above the lookup (Opus 5 $+0.0227$ with intervals $[-0.0170,+0.0682]$ and $[-0.0114,+0.0632]$); the random-token lookup falls to $0.4716$ and the count reader ties every pair.
An unrelated name in the list does raise the scores: the sibling version scores above the \present{} version for every reader but Opus 5 (the sibling-minus-\present{} mean for Haiku 4.5 $+0.0859$, both intervals clear of zero; Opus 5 $+0.0376$, intervals including zero), which is the list-length component the primary comparison removes.

\paragraph{GPT-4.1 mini on the expiry pairs under the all-states summary.}
Under the all-states summary GPT-4.1 mini reads retraction at $0.8725$ and expiry at $0.5869$ on this set, although both states are named in the same way.
The $30$ expiry triples show why, because the three versions of each triple can be read side by side.
The model scores its \pn{} version at or below $0.05$ on $26$ of the $30$ pairs, and the \present{} and \absent{} versions score the same low values, so the three versions are indistinguishable to it.
Only four pairs draw a score of $0.40$ or more on the \pn{} version, and in two of those the \present{} version draws the same score.
Every expiry question carries the upcoming intent, and every \pn{} version names the expired attribute in the expired-names section.
The model therefore does not take a name listed under the expired heading as evidence against a question about an upcoming event, whereas a name under the retracted heading moves it.
Appendix~\ref{app:resp} records the same asymmetry on the full population, where the expiry row falls under the all-states summary.

\paragraph{Three registered checks on this set.}
The list-length check pads the superseded-names list of each of the $661$ main-study summaries with correct attribute names that no scored question of the persona targets, to $15$, $30$ and $60$ names, and scores both lookups and GPT-4.1 mini at each length against the $1.75$-name row (Table~\ref{tab:pad}).
The embedding lookup falls from $0.7484$ to $0.6696$ at $60$ names, a difference whose item interval excludes zero and whose persona interval includes it; the lexical lookup is flat at every length; GPT-4.1 mini degrades first at $15$ names, reliably below $-0.05$ at $15$ and $30$.
%% Writing pass Phase 1 (2026-09-15), item 90: the store's-own-pool sentence from Section 5 (reviews/NUMBERS_R110.md AUX-R110-A4: 0.6531; 99.2 names; 129).
With every untargeted name the store holds on the list, a mean of $99$ names per summary and at most $129$, the embedding lookup reads $0.6531$; continued past that pool with other personas' names to $100$ and $200$ it reads $0.6558$ and $0.5960$ (Table~\ref{tab:pad}), so a longer list of the store's own names is untested.
%% R118 (the author's read of 19 September; the landing map's Limitations payer): the Limitations sentence moved here whole.
Beyond the store's own name pool, a mean of $99$ names, the lookup is tested only with other personas' names.
Its amendment injects wrong names into the counterfactual set's superseded-only lists at FR-Bank's measured rate of $0.232$ and at $0.40$: the lookup falls from $0.7663$ to $0.6740$ and $0.6001$, and GPT-4.1 mini and Haiku 4.5 fall with it and stay within $0.02$ of it on the same lists, every weaker interval including zero.
The paraphrase check asks each of the $148$ questions again in a wording that shares no content token with the attribute's slot path or with any attribute name the summary lists; it is reported after Table~\ref{tab:pad}.
The long-list check pads the superseded-names lists of this set's own $444$ summaries to $60$ names for the three lookups and all five models, with four predictions; it is reported after the paraphrase check.
%% R139 payer P10 (2026-09-22): Table 1's notes b and c, moved from its notes.
In Table~\ref{tab:cf-main}, the lookup with one count feature is a logistic reader over the name lookup's similarity and the guessed category's non-active share, fit with personas left out (Table~\ref{tab:cf-hybrid}); the two count readers read different quantities: the given-category reader ties every supersession triple and is perfect on the counted states by construction.
%% R139 payer P9 (2026-09-22): Table 2's per-row notes, moved from its caption.
Table~\ref{tab:robust} reads the pooled AUROC on the counterfactual set unless noted: rows 2 and 5 matched and post hoc (marked $^{p}$), row 5 on the $60$ counted-state pairs; row 3 the \pn{} version against its sibling, matched, on the $88$ supersession pairs; row 7 the $115$ reworded pairs; row 9 the $88$ supersession triples, the lists padded to $60$ names, Opus at one draw against its five-draw row; row 10 the full obligation set.

%% R134 (2026-09-21): the paraphrase check landed (registrations/ADDENDUM_PARA.md, registered text sha256 45cad63d..., descriptive; the home its section 9 names,
%% 'Appendix D.2', is this appendix). The prose is written by reviews/v23/tools/port_r134.py from cf2_paraphrase/results/para_printed.json and the tables by
%% cf2_paraphrase/tools/para_tex.py (spliced at the per-table markers by build_v23.py); every number through r134_number_gate.py; ledgers
%% cf2_paraphrase/NUMBERS_PARA.md (AUX-PARA-*) and reviews/NUMBERS_R134.md (AUX-R134-PRINTED). Report reviews/para/REPORT_PARA.md.
\paragraph{The paraphrase check.}
Each of the $148$ questions was rewritten by GPT-4.1 mini under a deterministic screen that forbids the attribute's own words and every name the summary prints; $24$ pairs had no rewrite that passed the screen after three drafts.
An author read the remaining $124$ against two questions, whether the rewrite asks the same thing under the same intent and whether it names the plan or value, and excluded $9$ on the first, leaving $115$ ($76$ supersession, $17$ retraction, $22$ expiry) whose labels transfer from the original triples because the store versions are unchanged.
The registered floor of $120$ accepted pairs was amended to the $115$ before any scoring call, nothing was revisited, and the $124$ are reported as a sensitivity analysis, not registered.
The registration is descriptive: it carries no prediction, only the branch readings of Table~\ref{tab:para-pooled} at a $\pm0.05$ tolerance.

The read was assisted.
An AI assistant pre-read the exported sheet and flagged rows with a one-line reason, and a coding session printed persona evidence for nine flagged rows; neither saw a reader's output or a score, and every verdict is an author's.
Against those verdicts, the rows the assistant flagged as ones it would have rejected were excluded $5$ of $5$, the rows it flagged as depending on the persona $3$ of $4$, the rows it flagged as vaguer but passable $1$ of $12$, and the unflagged rows $0$ of $103$.
The sheet, the flags, the evidence and every verdict are in the artifact.

On the named state, reworded, GPT-5.6 Luna is the only reader above the lookup (Table~\ref{tab:para-sup}), by $0.04$ on the pooled metric.
It is the reader added after the registration; its matched interval includes zero; its pooled lower bounds are $0.009$ and $0.004$; this is one of five comparisons and no multiplicity correction is applied; and Haiku 4.5's pooled and matched readings also disagree on this state.
On the original questions of the same pairs no reader is reliably above the lookup.

The registered readings, per summary, at the $\pm0.05$ tolerance on the point estimate: under the summary naming superseded records only, the lexical lookup collapses and the embedding lookup holds; GPT-4.1 mini and Opus 5 hold where the lexical lookup collapses, Haiku 4.5 collapses, and the two added readers hold.
Under the summary naming all states both lookups collapse and only Opus 5 holds; the embedding lookup's pooled loss there is beyond the registered tolerance, while its within-triple loss is not reliable (Table~\ref{tab:para-matched}).

The rewrites are systematically more generic than the originals.
Because the screen forbids the attribute's own words and every printed name, specific nouns give way to generic ones: ``What materials do I need to prepare for my upcoming meeting?'' became ``Which items should I get ready for the coming discussion?''; ``What type of headlamps am I currently carrying?'' became ``Can you tell me the model of the lights I currently possess?''; ``At this moment, how much am I paying per bag through the cooperative?'' became ``At present, what is the charge for every portion I get from the association?''.
Over the $115$ pairs the mean cosine of the question to its target name falls from $0.486$ to $0.317$ and the mean content-token overlap with it from $0.484$ to $0.000$, so every change above reflects both the loss of shared words and vaguer wording.

Draws: GPT-4.1 mini $1$, Haiku 4.5 $5$, Opus 5 $1$ against its recorded $5$, GPT-5.6 Luna and GPT-5.6 Sol $5$; the GPT-4.1 mini lane synchronous at list price, the Haiku 4.5 and Opus 5 lanes through the batch interface, the two added readers through the raw interface with the settings of their Table~\ref{tab:cf-main} rows.
Every served model identifier was checked before its lane, every lock of the registration held, and the lookup gate reproduced the recorded values on the original questions at four decimals.
An interim aggregate read was taken with the Opus 5 and Sol lanes then pending; no lane or analysis changed after it.

%% R137 (2026-09-22): the long-list check landed (registrations/ADDENDUM_CF_PAD.md, registered text sha256 a84dac1a..., frozen alone at 06c611f before the first paid call; run at 6d7f224).
%% The prose is written by reviews/v23/tools/port_r137.py from cf_pad/results/cfpad_printed.json and cf_pad_printed.json and the tables by cf_pad/tools/cfpad_tex.py (spliced at the per-table
%% markers by build_v23.py); every number through r137_number_gate.py; ledgers cf_pad/NUMBERS_CF_PAD.md (AUX-CFP-*) and reviews/NUMBERS_R137.md (AUX-R137-PRINTED). Report reviews/r136/REPORT_R136.md.
\paragraph{The long-list check.}
The superseded-names list of each of this set's $444$ summaries was padded with attribute names drawn from the persona's own printed name map, the pool being the names printed in all three versions of the triple less every name in any version's superseded section, every name a scored question of the persona targets in either population, and the change set; the pool holds $71$ to $121$ names per triple (median $102$), so no pad draws on other personas' names.
One pad is drawn per triple under a recorded seed and the triple's three versions receive the identical pad, so the \pn{} version's list has exactly $60$ names and its siblings' lists are shorter by the committed section alone ($57$ to $60$ names); the counts above the list are left as written, so the superseded total disagrees with the list length, as in the list-length check above.
The target is never in its own pad; its alphabetical rank in the \pn{} list is never first and last on $4$ of the $88$ supersession triples (minimum $2$, median $41.5$, maximum $60$; by tenths of the list, $5$, $3$, $2$, $5$, $8$, $12$, $12$, $9$, $9$, $23$).
The renderer reproduces every committed text byte for byte over the committed names alone ($444$ of $444$), the leak scan reports $0$ failures, the lookups reproduce their recorded values on the committed lists at four decimals, and the registration was frozen and committed before the first call, with both endpoints, the pooled AUROC and the matched score on the supersession triples, fixed in it.
Caps of \$$12$ (Anthropic) and \$$20$ (OpenAI) were metered from the rows at the page prices; the run spent \$$8.11$ and \$$15.54$, no cap was reached, no draw was dropped, and no deviation was recorded.

The four predictions, each read on the pooled AUROC of the supersession slice with the matched reading beside it:
P-L1, that each of GPT-5.6 Luna, GPT-5.6 Sol and Opus 5 reliably exceeds the name lookup on the $60$-name list, fails on Opus 5 (Luna $+0.1375$ and Sol $+0.1274$ above it with both intervals, Opus 5 $-0.0336$ with both intervals including zero; Table~\ref{tab:cfpad-minus-lookup}); P-L2, that neither GPT-4.1 mini nor Haiku 4.5 reliably exceeds it, holds ($-0.0525$ and $-0.0062$); P-L3, that the lookup loses more than $0.05$ against its unpadded row with both intervals below zero, holds ($-0.1807$; Table~\ref{tab:cfpad-sup}); P-L4, that each of the three stronger models loses less than the lookup at the point estimate, fails on Opus 5 (Luna $-0.0636$ and Sol $-0.0693$ lose less, Opus 5 $-0.2245$ loses more).
Luna and Sol are above the lookup on the matched metric as well, both intervals clear of zero (Table~\ref{tab:cfpad-minus-lookup}).

%% R139 (2026-09-22): Phase 1 of the brief 'KtS R139' (mock review 14, W2 and W1), post hoc: the equivalence test (reviews/r139/tools/equiv_r139.py) and the length-only reader
%% (length_r139.py), written by reviews/r139/tools/port_r139.py; ledger reviews/NUMBERS_R139.md (AUX-R139-EQUIV, -E-GROUPS, -B6, -LENGTH); every number through r139_number_gate.py.
\paragraph{Equivalence within the tolerance, post hoc.}\label{app:equiv}
A reliable difference is one shown to differ from zero; a difference that is not reliable is not thereby shown to be small. The mirror test is two one-sided tests at $5\%$: a difference is equivalent within $0.05$ when both of its $90\%$ intervals, the item-level (triple-level on the matched metric) and the persona-grouped one, lie inside $[-0.05,+0.05]$. The $90\%$ intervals come from the same $1{,}000$ draws that give the printed $95\%$ ones. Tables~\ref{tab:equiv} and~\ref{tab:equiv-para} apply the test to every model-minus-lookup row printed for the default summary, the sibling-name control, the all-states summary, the padded set and the paraphrase set, and to the lookup with one count feature against the three stronger readers. A row can be neither reliable nor equivalent; its difference is then neither shown nor excluded. The test is post hoc and changes no reliable verdict.
On the short list's supersession triples, on the matched metric, Luna, Sol and Opus are equivalent to the lookup, mini is reliably below it and Haiku is neither; pooled, mini is neither. Under the sibling-name control no reader is equivalent to the lookup, and the lookup with one count feature is neither reliably different from nor equivalent to any of the three stronger readers.

\paragraph{The length-only reader, post hoc.}\label{app:length}
The default lists are short: the \pn{} version's superseded list holds $2.6$ names on average and at most $6$ on the supersession triples, and $24$ of the $88$ \present{} lists are empty; at these lengths the lookup is close to a string match against one or two candidates (Table~\ref{tab:cf-emptiness}; FR-Bank's own lists in Appendix~\ref{app:census}).
A reader whose score is the number of names in the superseded-names section of the summary it is given, and nothing else, is scored as the count readers are (Table~\ref{tab:length}). On the supersession triples the \pn{} version's list is longer than each sibling's in every triple, on the default and the padded lists alike (by one name on $81$ triples, two on $6$ and three on $1$), so the reader wins all $88$ triples on both; on the padded lists, where every \pn{} list holds $60$ names and every sibling's fewer, it is also perfect pooled. On the sibling-name control the lists have equal length by construction and the reader ties every triple. The pad is identical across a triple, so the padded set carries the length difference the default lists already carry; the sibling padded to equal length is the control of the next paragraph (Table~\ref{tab:sb60}).

%% R141 (2026-09-24): the sibling-name control at 60 names (registrations: reviews/r141/registrations/ADDENDUM_R141_SB60.md, frozen alone at f224177 before the first call; the run 2588717; scored 7a62892; reviews/NUMBERS_R141.md AUX-R141-*), written by reviews/r141/tools/port_r141.py; every number through r141_number_gate.py.
\paragraph{The sibling-name control at $60$ names.}\label{app:sb60}
A fourth version of each of the $88$ supersession pairs takes the padded \pn{} text and swaps the target's name for a same-category sibling, so the two lists hold the same $60$ names but one, under an identical pad and an identical count table (Table~\ref{tab:sb60}). The sibling is the pair's sibling from the short-list control; where that name was already in the pad, which happened on $35$ pairs, a substitute was drawn from the pair's remaining eligible names under a recorded seed. The renderer reproduces every text byte for byte, every list holds $60$ distinct names, and the leak screen found $0$ of $30$ answers correct. Before the freeze the name lookup was recorded at $0.8239$ matched and the length-only reader, the count reader and the random-token lookup at $0.5000$. Of the four predictions, Luna reliably above the lookup came out otherwise (matched $+0.0398$, the persona interval reaching below zero; pooled $+0.1122$, reliable); Sol reliably above it held ($+0.0852$ matched, $+0.1238$ pooled); GPT-4.1 mini not reliably above it held ($-0.0909$ matched); and Luna and Sol at or above $0.80$ with the persona interval above $0.50$ held ($0.8636$ and $0.9091$). Seeds $43$ to $46$ change no reading. Luna, Sol and mini ran five draws each through the raw interface; Haiku 4.5 and Opus 5 were not run. Section~\ref{sec:lookup} words Luna's result as holding on the pooled metric only, a wording deviation from the registered branch clause (\emph{Luna's lead is not separated from the cue}): the registration promised the pooled reading beside the matched one, and the paper names the pooled metric wherever the two disagree.

\paragraph{The expiry state extended by a third descriptive draw.}
The candidate census behind the counterfactual draw (in the artifact) found $19$ expiry candidates that our read had passed and the draw had left undrawn, $14$ of them within the cap of four pairs per persona and the one-pair-per-name rule.
A third descriptive registration draws those $14$, builds their three store versions by the same construction, drafts their questions through the same renderer before any version exists, and scores the same readers under the same summaries; its questions and summaries pass the same leakage screen ($0$ of $14$ on every summary).
Table~\ref{tab:cf3-expiry} prints the extension beside the $444$'s expiry pairs and pools the two, $44$ pairs, with the pooling stated in the caption.
%% R123 Phase C: the Limitations clause on the extension draw moved here (the payer for the three added Limitations sentences).
The counted-state group stays small with it, $44$ expiry pairs against $88$ supersession pairs.
On the extension the all-states lookup reads expiry at $0.97$ and GPT-4.1 mini at $0.68$ under the all-states summary; retraction stays at $30$ pairs, because one candidate in ten passes our read for a cancellable plan ($36$ of $370$ read), and the candidate census in the artifact records every candidate with the rule that removed it.

\section{Monitoring-scoped instrument ledger}\label{app:ledger}

Every failure in the ledger is the same kind, a semantic assumption hidden inside a step that looked mechanical, so the ledger is kept as one list.
% C108
The following ledger contains only measurement steps germane to E0, E1, the cross-model set, the question-signal audit, and their economics; measurement steps outside this paper's monitoring scope are excluded.
% C085, relocated verbatim from Section 7.1 (R87 B7, ORDERED-MECHANICAL).
The lesson: printing a quantity beside the rule that constrains it makes the redundancy a detector for exactly this kind of failure.
The twelve entries of the ledger, each stating what went wrong and what changed, are in the supplement (Section~S.1), because each is a record rather than a result.
The supplement also carries the account of the four failures of the same kind in this manuscript's own verification battery.

\section{Experimental and reproducibility detail}\label{app:repro}

\subsection{Notation and study glossary}\label{app:glossary}

The paper uses one vocabulary and the artifact records another.
Three pairs of terms recur in the appendix prose before the table at the end of this section defines them: \emph{blind} and \emph{indexed} are the main text's question only and with the summary; \emph{route A} and \emph{route B} are the summary route and the tag route; and \emph{the monitor} is the reader, a name lookup or a model.
The two blocks below define every term the main text uses, and a third block in the supplement (Section~S.5) maps the internal names a reader meets in the registrations, ledgers and row files onto the paper's term.
The notation box and the definition of the lifecycle summary follow the two blocks, so that every symbol the appendices use is defined here.
Two labeled queries from the hiking persona illustrate Definition~\ref{def:coverage}.
Under the current intent ``What's David's family background?'' is \present{}.
Under the upcoming intent ``Does David have any upcoming exams to prepare students for?'' is \pn{}, because the store holds an exam record whose date has passed.

\begin{center}
\fbox{\begin{minipage}{0.97\linewidth}
\ttfamily\tiny
MEMORY STORE INDEX  (metadata only; no fact content)\\
entries in this store: 575\\[1mm]
CATEGORY\hspace{18mm} ENTRIES\quad ACTIVE\quad SUPRSD\quad RETRACT\quad EXPIRED\\
HOBBIES\_RECREATION\hspace{10mm}29\hspace{8mm}18\hspace{8mm}11\hspace{10mm}0\hspace{10mm}2\\

% from, frbank:david:FULL:BELIEVED, and are ledgered at AUX-R86-INDEX-EXCERPT.
AGES (days)\quad newest 1\quad oldest 518\quad median 258\quad bands 0-30: 47, 31-90: 54, 91-180: 75, 181-365: 229, 365+: 170\\[1mm]
ATTRIBUTE NAMES BY CATEGORY (value-scrubbed; abridged)\\
HOBBIES\_RECREATION: hiking\_experience, hiking\_preference, hiking\_preference\_scenery, ...\\
PROJECTS\_ENDEAVORS: camp\_stove\_status, project\_status, trip, trip\_plan, ...\\
ATTRIBUTE NAMES CARRYING SUPERSEDED CONTENT (abridged): hiking\_preference, ...
\end{minipage}}
\end{center}
\noindent\textit{This is what the indexed monitor reads}, an abridged, real FR-Bank summary that exposes counts and attribute names but no stored value.
The EXPIRED column is a flag derived from each entry's event time and the ACTIVE column counts the entries that are neither superseded nor retracted, so an expired entry can also be counted ACTIVE and the four lifecycle columns overlap; a record carrying more than one mark is labelled by the first of retracted, superseded, expired (Section~\ref{sec:framework}).

\begin{center}
\fbox{\begin{minipage}{0.97\linewidth}
\scriptsize
\textbf{The two judgments, and the label they are scored against.}\par\smallskip
\begin{tabular}{@{}p{.20\linewidth}p{.74\linewidth}@{}}
FOK & feeling of knowing: the score elicited as a probability that the store covers the query under its intent \\
NOK & negative-obligation knowledge: the score elicited as a probability that the store holds content whose lifecycle state forbids use \\
\pn{} & present-negative: the label for a query whose relevant content is present and would make the answer wrong if used \\
intent & the time frame a query asks about: \textsc{current}, \textsc{upcoming}, \textsc{past}, or \textsc{change} \\
lifecycle state & \textsc{active}, \textsc{superseded}, \textsc{expired}, or \textsc{retracted} \\
AUROC & the probability that a randomly chosen positive scores above a randomly chosen negative \\
ECE & expected calibration error: the gap between stated probabilities and observed frequencies \\
\end{tabular}
\end{minipage}}
\end{center}

\begin{center}
\fbox{\begin{minipage}{0.97\linewidth}
\scriptsize
\textbf{Conditions, stores, and populations.}\par\smallskip
\begin{tabular}{@{}p{.20\linewidth}p{.74\linewidth}@{}}
blind / indexed & the monitor sees the question only / also sees the metadata summary \\
the gain ($\Delta$) & the indexed score minus the blind score on the same questions \\
ground-truth / store-written summary & built from ground truth / from the fields the backend itself exposes \\
easy / hard tier & a whole category removed / a target missing inside a populated category \\
monitor call & one pre-retrieval request returning FOK and NOK \\
FR-Bank & the lifecycle-tracking store of \citet{fr}, the one evaluated store that records a lifecycle state on every entry \\
Mem0 & a production memory layer whose \texttt{UPDATE} overwrites history \\
A-MEM & an agentic memory system organized around linked notes \\
pilot / main study & the feasibility probe / the registered study \\
mini / luna / sol & \texttt{gpt-4.1-mini} / \texttt{gpt-5.6-luna} / \texttt{gpt-5.6-sol}, in ascending size and price \\
\end{tabular}
\end{minipage}}
\end{center}

%% prose, Section 3); Appendix E.6's carried U7 sentence refers to Definition~\ref{def:index}, which resolves here.
\begin{center}
\setlength{\fboxsep}{1.5pt}%% Phase 4c (L4): the frame and row spacing tightened; the words are unchanged
\fbox{\begin{minipage}{0.97\linewidth}
\scriptsize
\setlength{\tabcolsep}{1.5pt}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{0.92}
\noindent\textbf{Notation.} The terms below are used verbatim throughout.\par
\noindent\begin{tabular}[t]{@{}p{0.115\linewidth}p{0.36\linewidth}@{}}
\midrule
lifecycle state; non-active & the tag a record carries, \textsc{active}, \textsc{superseded}, \textsc{retracted} or \textsc{expired}; the last three are the non-active states \\
forbidding record & the non-active record that would make an answer wrong if used \\
intent; \pn{} & the time frame a question asks about (\textsc{current}, \textsc{upcoming}, \textsc{past}, \textsc{change}); present-negative: the store holds relevant content and using it would make the answer wrong. An \emph{obligation} is a question whose label is \pn{} \\
FOK; NOK & the coverage judgment, is the question covered; the obligation judgment, does the store hold anything not to use \\
summary & the count table (records per category and lifecycle state) and the name map (the attribute names each category exposes, values removed); the superseded-names list is the part of the name map that carries superseded content \\
\end{tabular}\hfill\begin{tabular}[t]{@{}p{0.115\linewidth}p{0.36\linewidth}@{}}
\midrule
names, counts & a summary \emph{names} a state when it lists the attribute names in that state and \emph{counts} it when it gives only a count of it \\
blind; indexed & the monitor sees the question alone; the monitor sees the question and the summary \\
ground-truth summary; store-written summary & the summary built from ground truth; the summary built from the fields the backend itself exposes \\
name lookup & the similarity lookup over the summary's superseded-names list, the baseline with no generative-model call that every model is measured against \\
padded list & a superseded-names list lengthened to a fixed size ($60$ names) with correct attribute names of the same store that no scored question targets, the counts left as written \\
\end{tabular}
\end{minipage}}
\end{center}
\begin{definition}[Lifecycle summary]\label{def:index}
% C035 (R77-T1, D5); the size claim qualified to a fixed name vocabulary.
A summary $M(S)$ is maintained outside query time. Ours pairs a count table with an attribute-name map from which every stored value is removed. The count table holds each category--lifecycle count and, per category, the count of updates that superseded an entry. The name map records which names each category exposes and which carry superseded content; the names that carry superseded content form the superseded-names list. The size $|M(S)|$ is $O(|C|\cdot|\Xi|+|N|)$ in the number $N$ of distinct names exposed, and for a fixed name vocabulary it is independent of the number of records $|S|$.
\end{definition}
%% R110: Definition 3 of v17 returns to vocabulary here (rule G3; READER_CLAIM.md section 2).
\noindent A summary \emph{names} a state when it lists the attribute names in that state and \emph{counts} it when it gives only a count of it; a reader \emph{reads} a state when its score separates that state's items from the rest.

\subsection{Pilot estimands}\label{app:pilot}

The pilot preceded the main study with a different prompt, so its values support the design lesson and cannot be compared numerically with the main study.
Its estimands and their table are in the supplement (Section~S.7).

\subsection{Main-study construction and checks}\label{app:construction}

This appendix carries the two user prompts, the obligation half of the rubric, and the construction record of the main study, because every reported estimate is bounded by one of them.
Listing~\ref{lst:summary} is the abridged summary the indexed monitor sees, rendered from the store of one persona.

%% R131 close, A1'' (2026-09-20): the provenance paragraph, every count from reviews/v22/tools/r131_close_gate.py (PROV; counted from the tree: LifeMemEval/, the freeze 5b097fb,
%% e1_metamemory/build/items_final.json, r82_counterfactual/cf2 and cf3, cf_scores_ix.json, cf2_paraphrase, e1xv_crossvendor/manifests/cell_84.json, r85_graphiti); reviews/NUMBERS_R131.md AUX-R131-CLOSE-PROV.
\paragraph{Provenance of the personas, the stores and the questions.}
The persona bank, the three main stores and the questions of the main study come from the FR-Bank release \citep{fr}; the question sets this study added were drafted by language models.
The release holds $40$ personas, each with a human-written manifest and $35$ language-model-generated sessions ($1{,}400$ in all), the FR-Bank, Mem0 and A-MEM stores built from those sessions, and $516$ questions.
This study asks $187$ of those questions of the full FR-Bank store and its two ablated variants, the category-removed and the slot-removed store ($178$, $176$ and $162$ questions; $516$ of the $661$ items of the full obligation set).
It adds the $145$ natural-absent probes, drafted by a language model and verified against the manifests.
GPT-4.1 mini drafted, through frozen renderers, the counterfactual set's $148$ questions with their three store versions ($444$ items) and the expiry extension ($14$ pairs, $42$ items).
It also drafted the $113$ crossed intent questions asked of $118$ drafted and the $148$ paraphrases.
The cross-vendor set is $84$ of the $661$ items ($76$ with a released question, $8$ probes) and the question-only probes are classifiers over the existing question strings, so neither adds a question.
The Graphiti graphs, one per persona, were built by the unmodified release from $1{,}365$ episodes rendered from the FR-Bank store's records, one per session, with every lifecycle field removed (Appendix~\ref{app:posthoc-graphiti}).

%% R126 addendum A5 (2026-09-20): Section 3's two summary-size sentences moved here.
Summary size grows with categories, states and distinct names; at a fixed vocabulary, it does not grow with record count. Counts remain because the design fixed them before the study.

%% R128 B1 (2026-09-20): the coverage half and the reply schema, verbatim from e1_metamemory/tools/prompts_e1.py (frozen; PREREG_E1.md records its sha256); one call elicits both judgments.
One call elicits both judgments. The coverage half of the rubric read in full: ``FOK (feeling of knowing): the probability, 0 to 100, that the store contains content relevant to this question -- anything that should shape the answer.'' The reply is one JSON object, \texttt{\{"fok": <0-100>, "nok\_candidates\_found": <true|false>, "nok": <0-100>, "would\_search": <true|false>\}}, and the system text adds that the two judgments are independent (``A store can hold plenty of relevant content with nothing stale in it, and it can hold stale content on a topic it otherwise covers poorly.'') and that the model must not attempt to answer the question; \texttt{would\_search} is not scored.
The obligation half of the elicitation rubric, which Section~\ref{sec:setup} points to, read in full:
``NEGATIVE OBLIGATION: content the store still holds whose correct influence on the answer is SUPPRESSION rather than use, because it has been superseded, retracted, or has expired. Judge this in two steps. Step 1, nok\_candidates\_found: is there content on this topic in the store that has been superseded by a later value, retracted by the user, or has passed its date? true or false. Step 2, nok: the probability, 0 to 100, that such content is present.'' Step 2 is the scored quantity.
 %% R123: the E.3 sentences of HELD_TEXTS_R121.md section 2 (AUX-CF2-IX-*).
For four of the five models the main-study rubric does not elicit the intent condition; Opus 5 applies it unprompted.
%% R126 (2026-09-20), Phase 4.6: the sentence corrected to what tab:e3-intent shows (reviews/v22/results/e3_intent_table.json); flagged for the author's check in REPORT_R126.md.
In the main populations every \pn{} question has current or upcoming intent by construction (Table~\ref{tab:e3-intent}), but a staleness judgment and an obligation judgment do not coincide even there: $44$ of the $174$ \present{} questions hold a non-active record of their own attribute, $33$ under current or upcoming intent, where the label follows the record the question targets, and $11$ under change intent, where intent licenses the old content; the crossover of Appendix~\ref{app:counterfactual} is where the two judgments are separated on one store.

Table~\ref{tab:e3-groups} scores the $104$ obligations against each of the three \present{} groups.
Group (a)'s non-active record is an unrelated fact under the same broad key, so the labels stand: for ``Does Bruna have any upcoming events or deadlines?'' the expired record under \texttt{event} is a marathon she had already run, while the conference she is to present at is active, and for ``What's Elena's family background?'' the expired record under \texttt{family\_member} is a sister's birthday reminder, while the family facts are active.
Every model separates the obligations from group (a) less well than from group (c), $0.57$ to $0.68$ against $0.69$ to $0.83$ with the summary.
Question only, the three models that score anything without a summary already rank group (a) above the obligations ($0.29$ to $0.40$, below chance), and the summary reverses that reliably without closing the gap; the name lookup, which reads no question, separates the obligations from each group about equally ($0.74$, $0.69$ and $0.74$).

A five-level scale in place of the free number scores within noise of the free-number format (Table~\ref{tab:e1-construction}), a descriptive comparison because we ran no equivalence test.
On FR-Bank the store-written summary keeps $101.4\%$ of the ground-truth summary's coverage benefit despite severe metadata error, and the excess over $100\%$ is within what sampling alone produces.
The $60\%$ retention threshold was fixed in advance at $60\%$ in the main study's preregistration (day $0$ of the registration record, Appendix~\ref{app:registration}) for coverage retention and was inherited unchanged when obligation retention was scored, before that figure existed.

The presence control's residual error is $\varepsilon=0.005$, the rate at which the check misses a target that is present.
Turning that into the chance an \absent{} claim is wrong also needs the base rate, which is $0.917$ present among resolved targets.
At that base rate the chance is $5.2\%$, roughly ten times $\varepsilon$, because the sampled questions are answerability-skewed.
Splitting the hard-question negatives by kind gives the two coverage results the main text rounds.
Against facts the user never had, coverage discrimination improves from $0.5517$ to $0.8782$ under the summary.
Against records the store's own operation deleted it moves from $0.4336$ to $0.4972$ and stays at chance.
A mislabeled \absent{} item enters the negative class in both conditions and is expected to shrink the measured discrimination, so the reported gains are conservative with respect to this error.

The wrong-summary control replaces only the summary text and holds the question, prompts, protocol and populations fixed (Table~\ref{tab:wrongindex}).
Giving the monitor another persona's summary costs it the whole benefit and inverts coverage below chance, from $0.7003$ to $0.3631$, so a mismatched summary actively misleads.
A shuffle of the categories, by contrast, leaves both judgments where they were.
% U7 (CLAIMS.md untouchable, byte-identical), placed under Table 14's footnote on
% 2026-08-30. R86 item 2.4.7 deleted the lead-in line and the CLAIMS-machinery framing.
% The U7 sentence itself is UNTOUCHABLE and stays: this is its only byte-identical
% instance in the document, and deleting it would fail claims_diff (D-R86-4).
Permuting which counts and attribute-name lists belong to which category changes neither judgment, which bounds the mechanism rather than that specificity: per-category correspondence is not necessary on FR-Bank, consistent with the name map rather than the count structure carrying the effect (Definition~\ref{def:index}), though the shuffle preserves the global name and count multiset and the flat superseded-names section and so bounds correspondence alone.

Giving the monitor another persona's summary costs it the whole benefit and inverts FOK below chance. Permuting which counts and which attribute-name lists belong to which category costs it nothing. $\Delta$ is the changed condition minus the original, so a negative number means the change hurt.}
\label{tab:wrongindex}
\centering

\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}

Manipulation & Judgment & $n$ & Intact & Manipulated & $\Delta$ & $95\%$ CI \\
\midrule

Another persona's summary & NOK & $661$ & $0.7339$ & $\mathbf{0.4659}$ & $-0.2668$ & $[-0.3298,-0.2010]$ \\
\addlinespace

\multicolumn{7}{@{}p{0.96\linewidth}@{}}{\footnotesize The shuffle permutes the per-category count rows and the per-category attribute-name lists under a fixed seed, preserving byte length exactly on all $661$ summaries and leaving column alignment intact, so the manipulation is correspondence rather than damaged formatting. It preserves the global multiset of names and counts and leaves the flat superseded-names section untouched, so it bounds per-category correspondence and nothing more. Each $\Delta$ is the recorded paired-bootstrap estimate of manipulated minus intact ($1{,}000$ item-level draws, seed $42$; single-class draws discarded) rather than the difference of the two displayed columns, from which it differs by Monte Carlo error only, at most $0.002$ on every row.}\\

\subsection{Statistics and serialization}

% C142
FOK AUROC treats non-\absent{} coverage as positive, while NOK AUROC treats \pn{} as positive.
Items are nested within the $40$ personas.
%% R139 Q8 (2026-09-23): A2's second clause, moved from Section 4.
Each registered family of model-minus-lookup comparisons holds five readers (three under the all-states summary).
% C143
Reported $95\%$ confidence intervals on paired AUROC differences use the fixed scorers' paired percentile bootstrap \citep{efron1993}, preserved in the anonymous artifact.
Within each reported row, $1{,}000$ item-level resamples are drawn with replacement under fixed seed $42$, with paired-condition scores moving together.
AUROC differences are recomputed per draw, single-class draws are discarded, and the $2.5$th and $97.5$th percentiles of retained differences form the interval.
A whole row containing one outcome class has undefined AUROC and no interval, so the mechanism probe reports mean coverage for such external-backend strata.
Ladder one-sample AUROC intervals use the same item-level draw-and-discard rule, so the scored item rather than the persona is the implemented resampling unit.
A persona-clustered sensitivity \citep{cameron2008}, resampling whole personas under the same paired draw-and-discard rule, was computed after the item-level records were frozen.
The registered item-level interval remains the primary endpoint.
The measured intracluster correlation of the paired indexed$-$blind contribution spans $[-0.0544,+0.0870]$, and the clustered-to-jackknife width ratio is $0.95$--$0.99$ on all six calibration rows.
Two independent cluster-level variance estimators therefore agree, and four of six clustered intervals are narrower than their item-level counterparts (Table~\ref{tab:cluster-sensitivity}).
% C144
Exact model identifiers were pinned, prompts were hash-verified, and the anonymous artifact preserves the exact prompts and serialized summaries used in monitor calls.

% R108: paper_v11.tex lines 1551-1562 carried inline so that the factorial's main effects carry the intervals recomputed from e2_metamemory/m1_factorial_raw.json (reviews/NUMBERS_R108.md AUX-R108-FACTORIAL).
\subsection{Factorial attribution}\label{app:factorial}

The factorial separates the lifecycle-index change from the two prompt changes and checks the main-study reproduction.
The lifecycle-index main effect is $+0.1980$, with intervals $[+0.1522,+0.2423]$ at item level and $[+0.1297,+0.2639]$ grouped by persona; the two prompt-fix effects are $+0.0134$ ($[-0.0105,+0.0354]$; $[-0.0073,+0.0348]$) and $-0.0193$ ($[-0.0451,+0.0034]$; $[-0.0396,+0.0011]$), both including zero.
The matching factorial condition reproduces the main study at $0.7344$ against its indexed NOK of $0.7339$; the intervals are recomputed from the recorded rows of the factorial, every cell rescored on the same bootstrap draw.

%% CHANGED (R92-1): the attribution paragraph relocated from Section 4.4 (CL-C060-C062); the section pointer
%% it carried is dropped here, where it would point at itself.
After the pilot's null obligation result the index gained its lifecycle columns and the prompt gained worked examples and a detection step, so the NOK redesign changed three things at once.
The attribution comparison estimates the effect of each of the three changes.
The lifecycle-index effect dominates both prompt effects, so the recovery is attributed to the lifecycle information, within the conditions we varied.

\subsection{Structureless-store inversion}\label{app:inversion}

% U2 relocated to F.7 on 2026-08-30: its evidence is the two inversion tables below.
Scope: the negative index effect is measured; the single-channel ablations do not identify its cause.

% U3 (CLAIMS.md untouchable, byte-identical), relocated from Section 6 on
% 2026-08-30 under the U-BLOCK RELOCATIONS ruling as extended that date.
% Section 6 states the open mechanism once; its two further statements
% are kept verbatim here beside the inversion tables that evidence them.
The association does not identify which rendered feature causes the inversion.
The candidate mechanisms in Section~\ref{sec:inversion} remain unresolved after the registered single-channel probe.

%% Writing pass Phase 1 (2026-09-15), item 1: the body's structureless-store paragraph cut to four sentences; its compression reading and the U1 disclosure (C071, byte-identical; pinned once) stand here.
%% R128 B2 (2026-09-20): the explanation corrected to what tab:dist shows; compression alone cannot move an AUROC (an order-preserving transform).
What the summary does to the score is a collapse onto a few low values, with the positives averaging slightly below the negatives (Table~\ref{tab:dist}).
%% R139 Q2 (2026-09-23, the author's approval): the pinned claim U5, moved verbatim from Section 6.
Scores collapse onto a few low values, with positives slightly below negatives, and the trigger remains unresolved.
% C071 (U1, byte-identical)
This is an inversion at every scale, not a below-chance score at every scale.
What triggers the collapse is unresolved, and the ablations that narrowed it follow.
%% R112 (2026-09-15), review-2 A1 (the author's ruling): the constrained rendering named where the verbatim summaries show it;
%% the store-written deltas and intervals are AUX-WP-A1-BELIEVED-BLIND (reviews/NUMBERS_WP.md section 6; reviews/v19/results/believed_blind_a1.json).
On these two stores the ground-truth summary is the constrained rendering: the manifest's lifecycle totals, with every name section printed as not expressible (the verbatim summaries below).
The store-written summary carries the store's own fields alone, a superseded count on Mem0 and recency on A-MEM.
The loss is measured on the constrained rendering.
The store-written summary is within tolerance of blind: $-0.0005$ on Mem0, intervals $[-0.0663,+0.0644]$ item-level and $[-0.0672,+0.0635]$ persona-grouped, and $-0.0313$ on A-MEM, $[-0.0983,+0.0400]$ and $[-0.0798,+0.0208]$ (Table~\ref{tab:baselines}).
%% R128 B3 (2026-09-20): the pooled 66's sampling (reviews/NUMBERS_R128.md AUX-R128-POOLED66; ADDENDUM_E1_5.md Run A).
The pooled $66$ external questions of its GPT-5.6 rows are the Mem0 and A-MEM part of a $150$-item stratified subsample of the main study's universe (seed $42$; quota $50$ present, $60$ absent, $40$ obligations, which resolves by backend to FR-Bank $84$, Mem0 $29$, A-MEM $37$): $15$ Mem0 and $21$ A-MEM questions on the full stores, all \present{}, and $14$ and $16$ natural-absent probes, all \absent{}; $36$ positives and $30$ negatives over $29$ personas; no obligation, since the obligation labels are FR-Bank's.

The inversion table collects the backend and model rows behind the cross-scale claim, so that the reading below can be checked row by row.
Section~\ref{sec:backends} reads one generalization from them: the benefit appears on the backend that carries lifecycle structure and inverts on the two that do not.

% outranks style, so the sentence moves rather than being rewritten. It still appears exactly
% once in the document, which is the level slogan_scan's pinned check counts at.
This law is directional but not mechanistic: the source of the observed below-chance behavior remains open.

% R103 (Q4 probe; registrations/ADDENDUM_Q4.md frozen sha256 8b6ee9e3..., descriptive; every value from q4_inversion_probe/NUMBERS_Q4.md
% AUX-Q4-M0 and AUX-Q4-AM). One arm, the recorded items, no prediction.
Whether the marker itself carries the inversion is a separate question. A descriptive probe left every summary byte-identical and appended one instruction line inside it: treat any field marked not expressible by this backend as absent, because the backend does not record it.
GPT-4.1 mini re-read the $522$ recorded items under that line, with the blind and intact rows read from the recorded records (Table~\ref{tab:q4-marker}).
Both stores move up to about $0.54$, and the difference from the intact summary excludes zero on A-MEM under both intervals and includes it on Mem0. On neither store does the score then differ from blind by an interval that excludes zero.
Grouped by persona the intervals read $[-0.0149,+0.1292]$ (Mem0) and $[+0.0285,+0.1403]$ (A-MEM) for the difference from the intact summary, and $[-0.1026,+0.0294]$ and $[-0.0739,+0.0327]$ for the difference from blind.
The probe carried no prediction and is reported whichever way it came out. It does not resolve the mechanism, because the score stays below blind at the point estimate on both stores. The instruction's word ``absent'' is also the name of a label, so the probe cannot separate a reading of the marker from a reading of the label.

% R97-1c (SIDE registration S1; Amendment 1): the registered appendix table. Every value is side_experiments/NUMBERS_SIDE.md
% sections 1 (spot-check), 2 and 4 (map), 6 (leakage), 7 (NB-4, NB-5) and 8 (AUX-SIDE-S1-MINI-AMEM, -MEM0); transcribed, not
% recomputed. The frozen held readings and the Mem0 descriptive text print once, below in this appendix (R102: moved from Section 5.3). CLAIMS.md R97 block.
A registered follow-up asked whether the inversion is a property of the store or of the summary it was handed.
Each stored entry of the two structureless stores was mapped to the manifest slot it holds, by exact lexical match and by embedding similarity. The map was checked by hand on thirty pairs per store: $24$ of $30$ correct on A-MEM ($0.800$) and $17$ of $30$ on Mem0 ($0.567$).
The Mem0 result fell below the pre-specified bar of $0.80$, so its map was restricted to lexically supported pairs, which cover $4{,}809$ of its $9{,}736$ entries ($49.4\%$), below the registered coverage floor of one half. A-MEM's map covers $10{,}974$ of $11{,}319$ entries ($0.970$).
From each map a name-bearing summary was rendered in the paper's summary format, with the per-category attribute names and the superseded-names section filled from the manifest for the mapped entries, and every stored value removed. The leakage screen scored $0$ of $30$ on both stores.
The recorded coverage items of each store were then scored by the same monitor against that summary, with the blind and ground-truth rows read from the recorded records (Table~\ref{tab:s1-names}).

A-MEM carries the two registered predictions and both hold (Section~\ref{sec:backends}); Mem0's summary covers $49.4\%$ of its entries, below the coverage floor of one half, so its row is descriptive and carries no verdict. On the paper's own summary of these stores the two lookups sit at $0.5000$ (Table~\ref{tab:baselines}), because that summary names nothing. On the name-bearing summary they reach $0.7000$ and $0.8554$ on A-MEM and $0.7104$ and $0.8700$ on Mem0.
The name lookup was not scored on these summaries, whose superseded-names section is thin by construction.

% R102 (2026-09-13): the SIDE registration's frozen S1-P1 held reading and the Mem0 descriptive text, moved here from Section 5.3 under the author's ruling R-d (only [MAIN]-marked frozen texts stay in the body); byte-identical to v9 L661-663.
The two readings written before the run, reported as they were preregistered: Given the manifest's names for what it holds, the store no longer inverts the benefit by the margin it did; the inversion was a property of the index the store was handed, not of the store alone.
The name-bearing summary does not invert on either store.
On Mem0 the name-bearing index could be built for $49.4$ percent of the store's entries under the lexical-required mapping the spot-check forced (two-channel precision $0.567$); below the registered coverage floor of one half, its coverage gain of $+0.3006$ (interval $[+0.2208,+0.3728]$; \trueidx{} $-0.0969$) is reported and carries no verdict.

\FloatBarrier
% R77-B: transcription of the committed E1-R baseline suite (AUX-E1R-BASELINES,
% e1r_robustness/NUMBERS_E1R.md SS6, artifact results/baselines_final.json). Zero recomputation.

\paragraph{What the summary does to the score.}
The recorded coverage scores on the two structureless stores show what the inversion consists of (Table~\ref{tab:dist}).
Blind, GPT-4.1 mini's score takes six distinct values with a variance of $0.0245$ on Mem0 and $0.0242$ on A-MEM.
Indexed under the constrained rendering it takes five values with a variance of $0.0060$ and $0.0052$, and the mean falls from $0.22$ to $0.17$ on Mem0 and from $0.22$ to $0.16$ on A-MEM.
The summary lowers the score on $86$ Mem0 items and raises it on $46$, and lowers it on $110$ A-MEM items and raises it on $48$, while the mean on positives and on negatives moves together.
Compression alone cannot lower an AUROC below $0.50$, because AUROC is unchanged by any order-preserving transform; what the rows show is a collapse onto five values in which the positives average slightly below the negatives ($0.1617$ against $0.1688$ on Mem0, $0.1544$ against $0.1658$ on A-MEM), and that ordering is the inversion.
The store-written summaries, which carry no not-expressible line, show no loss (within tolerance of blind, above), and the name-bearing summary rebuilt from ground truth restores coverage (Table~\ref{tab:s1-names}), so the loss belongs to the constrained rendering; which line of it produces the collapse is the question the ablations above leave open, and the marker's wording is not it (Table~\ref{tab:marker}).

\FloatBarrier
\subsection{Non-LLM baseline suite}\label{app:baselines}

The non-model readers of the summary carry numbered names in the registrations, and the main text calls the sixth the name lookup.
The registrations number them NB-1 to NB-6.
This appendix carries the whole family, the per-state split of the name lookup against the models, and the lookup on the coverage tiers, because each bounds a claim the main text makes about what a model adds.
Both readings were registered, the whole-population comparison as a sentence pair and the per-state one as a single reading.

Six non-model readers were run on the same serialized summaries the monitor reads, to ask whether a language model is needed at all.
The first is a deterministic lifecycle rule that parses per-category entry and lifecycle counts and assigns the question to a category by content-token overlap with that category's attribute names.
It scores coverage as the assigned category's share of the store's largest category and obligation as its superseded-plus-retracted-plus-expired share.
The second is a logistic model over numeric summary features only, label-supervised and persona-grouped.
The fourth and fifth are unsupervised query-to-summary lookups, lexical and embedding respectively, scored against the whole per-category name map.
The sixth is that same pair of lookups aimed at one section instead, the flat list under \texttt{ATTRIBUTE NAMES CARRYING SUPERSEDED CONTENT}, whose populations, tolerance and outcome sentences were fixed before it was scored.
The third was disqualified before scoring, because its features concatenated the summary with the question and its ablation showed question-only text outscoring the concatenated version on every population.
It read question templates rather than the store (supplement Section~S.1, item 12), and its column below is the summary-only ablation, which is the part that reads the store.

Three readings carry beyond the table, because each bears on a claim the main text makes.
The first is the store-written obligation row, where the monitor at $0.5959$ is indistinguishable from the embedding lookup at $0.5974$, a difference of $-0.0015$ inside the tolerance: under corrupted self-description the monitor's obligation judgment falls back to what a similarity lookup already achieves, which is the same degradation the $42.3\%$ retention records in Section~\ref{sec:backends}, reached by an independent route.
The second is that the monitor's coverage margin over the whole-map readers, the lookups that score the question against the whole name map, is not uniform across backends.
It exists where lifecycle structure exists and vanishes where it does not, which is the same boundary the inversion result draws.
The third is the structureless pair, where the lexical and embedding lookups sit at $0.5000$ and the other readers scatter around chance ($0.4184$ to $0.5226$).
The monitor's indexed point estimates under the constrained rendering fall below them ($0.4777$, $0.4526$; Section~\ref{sec:backends}).

% AUX-E1R-NB6 (R81). U9 FIREWALL: this paragraph reports what NB-6 measures. It does NOT
% join the BELIEVED retention figure to the flat-lookup bound, and no connective here may
% be read as doing so. The 42.3% figure is deliberately absent from this paragraph.
The name lookup was registered as a direct test of one exposure: whether the obligation judgment under a ground-truth summary is close to reading the answer off the superseded-names section.
The whole-map lookups cannot detect that, because they dilute the section across the whole name map.
Under the ground-truth summary it reaches $0.7484$ against the monitor's $0.7339$, so on this population the monitor holds no margin over that reader.
The difference is inside the $\pm0.05$ tolerance, so it does not establish the lookup as the better judge either; what it removes is the claim of a margin.
Under the store-written summary it reaches $0.5858$ against the monitor's $0.5959$, also inside the tolerance.
The whole-population difference under the ground-truth summary, monitor minus lookup, is $-0.0145$ with item-level interval $[-0.0769,+0.0549]$ and persona-clustered interval $[-0.1080,+0.0765]$ (Table~\ref{tab:nb6-composition}, last row), so the interval and the tolerance reading agree.
The ground-truth superseded list averages $1.75$ names per item and the store-written list averages $15.04$, so the two summaries give the lookup very different amounts of text to work with.
Mem0 and A-MEM were not scored by the lookup, because their superseded-names section carries only the marker the renderer writes where a store exposes no field, and the lookup would measure the marker rather than the store.
%% ADDENDUM_R81 reproduction check [APPX] F.8, one sentence; frozen wording ships verbatim.
On the coverage rows NB-6 scores the whole per-category name map and reproduces NB-4 and NB-5 to four decimals, so the coverage cells print a dash rather than repeating them.
The lookup has one descriptive value on the cross-model set, whole-set and per-state values on the store-counterfactual set, and none on the authorship-matched set. The comparison between model and lookup is therefore complete on the full obligation population ($661$) and on the store-counterfactual set ($444$).
%% R131 close, W7 (2026-09-20): the body's sentence moves here as a pointer (Appendix C.2 prints the intervals, tab:cf-fok).
On the counterfactual set every model's coverage interval excludes chance (Table~\ref{tab:cf-fok}, Appendix~\ref{app:counterfactual}).
Table~\ref{tab:cov-lookup} below adds the superseded section scored on the coverage rows, a measurement added after the main study.

The registered texts for these rows (Appendix~\ref{app:registration}) read as follows.
%% NB6-1 FAILS [MAIN] (registrations/ADDENDUM_R81.md section 6; AUX-E1R-NB6): frozen wording ships verbatim; moved here from Section 8 under
%% registrations/AMENDMENT_MAIN_PLACEMENT.md (R105, 2026-09-14; pending the author's ratification).
A matcher that scores each question directly against the store's list of names marked superseded reaches $0.7484$ on the same $661$ questions, against the monitor's $0.7339$.
Under a truthful summary the obligation judgment is available to a direct lookup on that one list.
%% AUX-E1R-NB6-PERSTATE, frozen reading (i) [MAIN] (ADDENDUM_R81 section 10.3 @ e8ba22c): ships verbatim; moved here from Section 8 (same amendment).
The superseded-names matcher matches the smallest model tested. Larger models score higher on the states the index only counts, an advantage the paired blind comparison does not attribute to the index (Tables~\ref{tab:nb6-attrib} and~\ref{tab:nb6-frontier}).
%% NB6-2 HOLDS [MAIN] (ADDENDUM_R81 Amendment 1; AUX-E1R-NB6): ships verbatim; moved here from Section 7 (same amendment). U9 FIREWALL: no connective
%% joins these two sentences to any retention sentence; the 42.3 percent figure is deliberately absent from this paragraph.
Under the metadata the backend actually exposes, the monitor's obligation judgment is no better than a matcher that reads the store's list of superseded names directly.
The monitor scores $0.5959$ on that row and the matcher $0.5858$ (Appendix~\ref{app:baselines}).

A census run after that result asks why the lookup works where it does, because the answer says what a reader can take from this summary and what it cannot.
The summary names attributes in exactly one place, the superseded-names section, and expiry and retraction reach the summary as counts and are never named.
Splitting the obligation items by the lifecycle state each obligation arises from reproduces that asymmetry exactly (Table~\ref{tab:nb6-composition}).
The lookup reaches $0.9534$ on supersession-derived items and sits at $0.4823$ on expiry and $0.4053$ on retraction, the last two at or below chance.
The monitor is weaker off supersession as well, at $0.5110$ and $0.5638$, and on retraction it is the higher of the two readers.
A check of whether each obligation's own attribute name appears in the section the lookup reads finds it for $74$ of $104$ under exact slot match: $63$ of $63$ supersession-derived, $11$ of $26$ retraction-derived, and none of the $15$ expiry-derived.
On the state the summary names, the lookup has the item's own name to read; whether the monitor reads it is a separate question that this census does not settle.
Where the self-description names an obligation, a lookup reaches it; where the self-description only counts it, the lookup does not.
Composition follows the same line: obligations on the full population are $63$ of $104$ supersession-derived, against $19$ of $40$ on the cross-model set, where the lookup scores $0.6023$.

\paragraph{The superseded-section lookup on the coverage tiers.}
The coverage rows of Table~\ref{tab:baselines} score the whole name map.
Table~\ref{tab:cov-lookup} scores the superseded section alone on the same coverage populations, beside GPT-4.1 mini's recorded indexed coverage score.
On the hard tier every coverage positive is an obligation, so the embedding lookup on the superseded section reads $0.7864$ against the model's $0.7003$, and the difference of $-0.0861$ has intervals that include zero.
The FR-Bank easy tier has ordinary present records as its positives, and there the model reads $0.7835$ against the superseded-section lookup's $0.6083$, a reliable margin of $+0.1752$.

A lookup over the whole name map, the reader the coverage rows of Table~\ref{tab:baselines} score, was computed on the easy tier after the fact and specified before recomputation: it reads $0.7469$ (persona interval $[0.6866,0.8028]$), and the model's margin over it is $+0.0367$ with intervals $[-0.0127,+0.0861]$ and $[-0.0036,+0.0776]$, not reliable and within tolerance, so the coverage margin the superseded-section lookup showed does not survive the stronger reader with no generative-model call (Table~\ref{tab:cov-lookup}, last row).
Pooling the easy tier across backends drops the lookup to $0.4021$, because the structureless stores' sections carry only the marker, and the model's margin there is $+0.2315$.

\paragraph{The coverage judgment against its floor.}
%% R118 ruling 4 (2026-09-19): Section 8's coverage paragraph moved whole from the body; its pointer to this appendix now names the table. No number changes.
The coverage margin over the name lookup does not survive a lookup over the whole name map.
On the easy tier ($354$ questions), where the positives are ordinary present records, GPT-4.1 mini scores $0.78$ against the name lookup's $0.61$, a reliable margin.
%% R112 (2026-09-16), post hoc F1 (reviews/NUMBERS_WP.md section 10, AUX-WP-F1-WHOLEMAP): whole-map lookup 0.7469; model minus whole-map +0.0367 [-0.0127, +0.0861] / [-0.0036, +0.0776].
A lookup over every name in the map, computed post hoc, scores $0.75$ on the same questions, and the model's margin over it is $+0.04$ with interval $[-0.01,+0.09]$, which is not a reliable one (Table~\ref{tab:cov-lookup}).
On the hard tier every coverage positive is an obligation, so the coverage and obligation scores are one row, and the lookup's $0.79$ against the model's $0.70$ is not a reliable difference.
FOK detects a missing topic and not a single deleted record, which is NOK's target (Appendix~\ref{app:construction}).
The score ranks well and calibrates badly (Appendix~\ref{app:posthoc}).

\subsection{Census of the store-written state}\label{app:census}

Table~\ref{tab:census} in Section~\ref{sec:backends} carries the census behind the over-mark, overwrite and unresolved-links reading.
%% R123 Phase D (2026-09-19): the four counts near one hundred reconciled in one sentence (AUX-BELIEVED-FR: 109 events, approximately 102 explained; AUX-SIDE-S2-CENSUS: 104 TRUE-superseded entries, 40 active; the 104 obligations: 63 / 26 / 15).
Four counts near one hundred in that census are four different things: the manifests record $109$ supersession events across the forty personas; the ground-truth join explains about $102$ of the store's inactive entries; the manifest's supersessions touch $104$ stored entries, $40$ of them still active; and the $104$ obligations of the full obligation set are questions, $63$ supersession, $26$ retraction and $15$ expiry, not entries; the $72\%$ of Table~\ref{tab:census} is a share of the $109$ events ($78$ of them), not of the $104$ entries.
This appendix records the store-written superseded list, the renderer's cap, and the fourth-backend trial, because each bears on how much of the loss is the store's.

%% Writing pass Phase 1 (2026-09-15), items 52 and 90: the 5% provenance sentence from Section 4 and the body's Conflate row (AUX-SIDE-S2-CENSUS; the R109 write-path census) as prose.
\paragraph{Two census facts the body states in brief.}
The pass rate of $5\%$ that Section~\ref{sec:setup} cites for FR-Bank's mask is the released store's own figure, measured on its $40$ retraction questions with the mask in place \citep{fr}.
The store also conflates retraction with supersession: of $31$ entries marked retracted across the $40$ personas, $22$ share a slot key with another entry and $16$ carry a supersedes pointer, so the write path records retraction through the supersession mechanism and the backend's own metadata cannot separate retracted from superseded content.
The manifests record $40$ retraction events, one per persona, and the ground-truth join resolves $9$ of them to a retracted entry ($23$ entries).
%% R126 addendum A2 (2026-09-20): Table 2's caption notes moved here (the matching threshold; the two reproduction scripts; the conflation note is the sentence above).
The count of retained active assertions in Table~\ref{tab:census} depends on the stored-versus-obsolete matching threshold of the census, and the artifact's \texttt{repro\_mem0\_erase.py} and \texttt{repro\_amem\_links.py} reproduce the table's Mem0 and A-MEM rows from the frozen banks.
%% R112 (2026-09-15), review-2 A1: the Section 7 history-call sentence, moved here as the page swap the ruling named.
Mem0's per-memory history call, which keeps the prior text under a known identifier, was not consulted, so the overwrite reading is of the record as exposed and says nothing about the history log.

% R97-2b (SIDE registration S2; Amendment 1): the frozen census reading (registrations/ADDENDUM_SIDE.md section S2, 'prints regardless,
% appendix only'; ships verbatim, so the em dashes stand) and the S2-U quantities; NUMBERS_SIDE.md sections 5 and 8
% (AUX-SIDE-S2-CENSUS, -S2-CAP-RECALL, -S2-MINI); rounded forms in reviews/NUMBERS_R97.md. Section 6 carries the frozen branch text.
\paragraph{The store-written superseded list, and the renderer's cap.}
A registered follow-up asked whether a filter on the metadata FR-Bank itself exposes could recover the obligation gain the store-written summary loses.
Two candidate rules were fixed before any call, with a precision bar of $0.80$, and a census of the store decided it at no cost.
Two filters on the metadata FR-Bank exposes --- the store's own supersession pointers, alone and restricted to an active successor at least fourteen days later --- leave the superseded list at $15.04$ and $8.96$ names per item against $1.75$ under ground truth, at precision $0.035$ and $0.062$. The store records $5{,}968$ deactivations and the manifest $104$ supersessions, and on the fields the store exposes the two are indistinguishable. Forty of the $104$ are still active in the store. The believed-state loss is not a filtering problem.
The one condition that ran is descriptive: the recorded store-written store versions rendered with the per-category cap of fifteen names lifted.
The superseded-names section then runs to $32.55$ names per item against $15.04$ under the cap and $1.75$ under the ground-truth summary.
Its recall of the ground-truth names rises from $30.0\%$ to $66.6\%$ at precision $0.0348$ under the cap and $0.0357$ without it.
On the $661$ obligation questions the obligation AUROC reads blind $0.4946$, ground-truth $0.7339$, store-written $0.5959$, and store-written with the cap lifted $0.6513$.
The paired gains over blind are $+0.2393$ $[+0.1801,+0.2949]$, $+0.1012$ $[+0.0458,+0.1543]$ (persona-clustered $[+0.0217,+0.1744]$) and $+0.1567$ $[+0.0961,+0.2162]$ (persona-clustered $[+0.0552,+0.2536]$).
The lifted summary retains $65.5\%$ of the ground-truth gain, item-level $[44.6\%,87.2\%]$ and persona-clustered $[31.5\%,93.6\%]$, against the recorded $42.3\%$ under the cap.
The lifted-minus-capped difference is $+0.0554$, item-level $[+0.0110,+0.0992]$ and persona-clustered $[-0.0019,+0.1173]$, so the two intervals disagree and the wider one governs in Section~\ref{sec:backends}.
The three registered texts for this row (Appendix~\ref{app:registration}) read as follows.
%% SIDE S2 (registrations/ADDENDUM_SIDE.md section S2): the 42.3 percent sentence, ships verbatim; moved here from Section 7 under
%% registrations/AMENDMENT_MAIN_PLACEMENT.md (R105, 2026-09-14; pending the author's ratification).
Under the \believed{} index the NOK gain is $+0.1012$ ($95\%$ CI $[+0.0217,+0.1744]$, with questions grouped by user profile). That keeps $42.3\%$ of the \trueidx{} gain, short of the $60\%$ we had set as the threshold.
%% SIDE S2-U not-expected-branch text (ADDENDUM_SIDE.md section S2; AUX-SIDE-S2-MINI): ships verbatim; moved here from Section 7 (same amendment).
Lifting the cap on the believed list, which raises its recall of ground-truth superseded names from $30.0$ to $66.6$ percent at unchanged precision, retains $65.5$ percent of the \trueidx{} gain, at or above the $60$ percent threshold.
Part of the believed-state loss recorded at $42.3$ percent is an effect of the renderer's cap of fifteen names rather than of the store's metadata.
%% The main study's frozen verdict sentence on the BELIEVED obligation row (PREREG_E1; CLAIMS.md): ships verbatim; moved here from Section 7 (same amendment).
NOK degrades materially under believed metadata but remains positive.

%% R126 addendum A7 (2026-09-20): Section 6's 'Haiku fares better' paragraph moved here in the readable body's words (AUX-BH-SPLIT: 0.8658 / 0.8855 on 53; 0.6839 on 10).
Haiku 4.5 fares better where FR-Bank keeps the target's name: with the uncapped store-written summary it scores $0.87$ on the supersession obligations whose name survives, against $0.89$ under ground truth, and $0.68$ where the name is missing (Table~\ref{tab:bh-split}). This descriptive split resembles the target-name ablation, but the missing-name subgroup is small and its paired decline is not reliable.

\paragraph{Haiku 4.5 under the store's own metadata.}
%% R110: ADDENDUM_BELIEVED_H (frozen 257a090; believed_h/NUMBERS_BELIEVED_H.md AUX-BH-LOCK, -LOOKUP-LIFTED, -H45-15, -H45-LIFTED, -RETENTION-15, -RETENTION-LIFTED, -BRANCH, -SPLIT).
A descriptive registration scored Haiku 4.5 at five calls per question on the recorded store-written texts under both renderings, the capped ($15.04$ superseded names per item) and the lifted ($32.55$ names, at most $50$), against its recorded blind ($0.4150$) and ground-truth ($0.8210$) rows on the full obligation set, with the name lookup on the same lists ($0.5858$ capped, $0.6588$ lifted, persona interval $[0.5777,0.7319]$).
Haiku 4.5 reads $0.7069$ capped ($[0.6657,0.7504]$; $[0.6620,0.7541]$) and $0.7364$ lifted ($[0.6911,0.7858]$; $[0.6665,0.8024]$), $-0.1141$ and $-0.0847$ below its ground-truth row, both reliable, and $+0.1212$ ($[+0.0628,+0.1849]$; $[+0.0317,+0.2107]$) and $+0.0776$ ($[+0.0272,+0.1355]$; $[-0.0031,+0.1498]$) above the name lookup.
Its retention, the ratio of the paired resampled differences, is $0.7190$ capped ($[0.6214,0.8497]$; $[0.5935,0.8603]$) and $0.7916$ lifted ($[0.6838,0.9243]$; $[0.6342,0.9425]$), at or above the sixty-percent line under both renderings.
The sixty-percent line was a conventional threshold, fixed before the main study, with no recorded reason for its value.
GPT-4.1 mini's retention figures above divide by an indexed-minus-blind difference that Appendix~\ref{app:laddertable} does not report as a gain, because its blind score is one value on $604$ of the $661$ questions, so Table~\ref{tab:levels} reports retention for Haiku 4.5 only.
Split by whether the obligation's own scrubbed name survives in the store-written list (Table~\ref{tab:bh-split}), Haiku 4.5 reads the $53$ supersession obligations whose name survives under the lifted rendering at $0.8658$ against $0.8855$ under the ground-truth summary ($-0.0197$, $[-0.0690,+0.0303]$; $[-0.0842,+0.0424]$), and the $10$ whose name is gone at $0.6839$ against $0.9122$ ($-0.2283$, $[-0.4288,-0.0430]$; $[-0.6568,+0.0182]$); under the cap the halves are $24$ and $39$, at $0.7993$ and $0.6933$.
